Phoenix's testimony says victim fell on top of his umbrella (umbrella under victim). That contradicts spatial relation: umbrella is below victim (consistent) but distance? In photo, umbrella is in a distance, not directly under. However proposition 5: broken power cable is above umbrella. In photo, cable above victim, umbrella far away. So cable above victim, not above umbrella. So contradiction: evidence says umbrella is not under victim (distance), but testimony says victim fell on top of umbrella (implies umbrella directly under victim). So which evidence? Evidence 2 (photo) or Evidence 5 (umbrella description). Evidence 5 is "Umbrella: Owned by the victim. Found broken near an electrical pole at the crime scene." Not spatial.

Thus contradiction between Evidence 2 (photo) and Testimony 3 (or 6). Let's pick Testimony 3: "After I shoved him, he... He fell down on top of his cheap umbrella."

Contradiction: Evidence 2 says umbrella is at a distance, not directly under victim. So spatial proposition: Umbrella is not below victim (i.e., not under). That would be opposite of Prop 4. So we can say proposition 4 is contradicted.

Thus answer: evidence 2, testimony 3.

**Step 1:**  
Testimony 4 (by Gravelle) states that the victim “tucked safely away” the letter in *her* left breast pocket. This implies that Ms. Crane was able to move and keep the letter on her person after being mortally wounded.

**Step 2:**  
Evidence 20 (Clothes Box) together with spatial propositions 3 and 4 says that *Ms. Crane is inside the Clothes Box* (“Ms. Crane is inside Clothes Box” and “Clothes Box contains Ms. Crane”). If she is inside a box, she cannot simultaneously have a letter in her left breast pocket.

**Step 3:**  
Proposition 3 (“Ms. Crane is inside Clothes Box”) and Proposition 4 (“Clothes Box contains Ms. Crane”) directly place Ms. Crane inside the box, contradicting the testimony that she was still able to hold a letter in her pocket.

**Step 4:**  
The spatial reasoning is straightforward: a person cannot be both *inside a closed container* (the box) and *wearing a letter in a pocket* at the same time. Therefore the location information from Evidence 20 (via the propositions) conflicts with Gravelle’s testimony about the letter.

**Step 5:**  
The contradictory pair is:

{"evidence": 20, "testimony": 4}

**Step 1:**  
The crime photo (Evidence 2) describes the umbrella as being “in a distance” from the victim, i.e., far away. Phoenix’s statement (Phoenix’s Testimony, listed as Evidence 6) says *“The victim fell on top of his umbrella.”* This places the victim directly on the umbrella, implying they are touching and the umbrella is not far.

**Step 2:**  
The spatial propositions that model these descriptions are:  
- Prop 5: *The umbrella is far from the victim.*  
- Prop 6: *The victim is far from the umbrella.*  
- Prop 3: *The umbrella is below the victim.* (or equivalently Prop 4: *The victim is above the umbrella.*)  

**Step 3:**  
Evidence 2 (and Evidence 5 about the umbrella’s location) support Props 5/6 (umbrella far from victim). Phoenix’s Testimony asserts Props 3/4 (victim directly on the umbrella). The two sets of propositions are mutually exclusive: an object cannot be both “far” and “directly beneath/above” another at the same time.

**Step 4:**  
Because the spatial relations conflict, the contradiction is between the physical layout shown in the crime photo (and the umbrella’s recorded position) and Phoenix’s claim that the victim fell onto the umbrella.

**Step 5:**  
JSON answer:

{"evidence":5,"testimony":6}

Lamiroir’s Testimony 4 says: “I saw nothing. The late Mr. LeTouse's body was behind a wall.” This asserts that she *did* see the body (behind a wall) from the doorway.

**Step 4:**  
The contradiction is identified by comparing the visual‑obstruction information (Evidence 10 / Prop 8) with Lamiroir’s claim of visual observation (Testimony 4). Since the obstruction prevents any line‑of‑sight, her claim of seeing the body is impossible, establishing the inconsistency.

This directly conflicts with Prop 13, which asserts the Empty Bottle is **not** on the Table in front of the Trailer. Hence the evidence (Empty Bottle on the table) contradicts the testimony (they were at that table) when combined with the spatial propositions.

No other evidence–testimony pair shows a clearer spatial inconsistency. The contradiction hinges on the location of the table and the presence of the Empty Bottle, as dictated by the propositions.

- **Prop 11:** “The forensic evidence claims Faraday and Rell were far from each other at the moment of attack.” (mirrored by Gumshoe’s observation of only a gunshot, indicating no close‑range struggle.)

**Step 3:**

The specific items embodying this are Evidence 2 (Det. Gumshoe’s testimony) and Testimony 2 (Franziska’s claim of simultaneous attack).

**Step 4:**
